# TSRP beta hardening report

**Reviewed:** 2026-10-13 (current evidence through 2026-10-13 22:31 UTC)

Concise release decision, findings ledger, and coverage matrix: [release readiness](release-readiness.md). This document is the detailed chronological evidence log.

## Reject conflicting SEC revenue aliases from the same filing — 2026-10-13 21:42 UTC

- Reproduced a source-selection ambiguity in `_sec_candidates()`: when two configured revenue concepts had different values for the same taxonomy, currency, start/end period, filing date, and accession, the selector silently kept the first alias. Those aliases are fallback concepts, not additive facts; their scopes can differ. The FASB taxonomy guide distinguishes customer-contract revenue from revenue presentation that can also include non-Topic-606 items such as lease revenue ([FASB guide](https://xbrl.fasb.org/impdocs/Rev2_TIG/Revenue.htm)); Ford's SEC filing illustrates a company with multiple revenue categories ([SEC filing](https://www.sec.gov/ixviewer/ix.html?doc=%2FArchives%2Fedgar%2Fdata%2F37996%2F000003799623000012%2Ff-20221231.htm)).
- Selection now excludes facts that conflict across aliases only when all identifiers match, including the filing accession, and keeps older unambiguous observations. This does not add aliases or combine revenue components. Added an order-independent selector regression and an active-analysis regression proving an ambiguous SEC value is not surfaced and Yahoo's matching-period value is used instead.
- Both focused regressions passed. Final warnings-as-errors backend suite: **426/426 passed** in **3.794s** under Python **3.12.15** with ephemeral localhost mock fixtures permitted; the first unprivileged attempt was blocked by sandbox denial on those five local-server tests and is not counted as a failure or pass. Python syntax checks for both changed Python files and `git diff --check` passed. No public providers were contacted; no score formula, data source, methodology version, UI, or deployment changed.
- Independent read-only review corroborated the cross-alias scope risk and regression gap. No code changes from the reviewer. Candidate remains detached at `e258c800666ca692785aaee495c4a54b001d3853` with **139** dirty/untracked entries; original checkout remains protected. No commit, push, Preview, or production change.

## Reject SEC long-term debt as an unqualified total — 2026-10-13 21:33 UTC

- Reproduced a second EV input substitution risk: if SEC supplied only a `LongTermDebt` or `LongTermDebtAndFinanceLeaseObligations` fact, the active analysis treated it as total debt. SEC filings distinguish short-term debt from long-term debt, and the `LongTermDebt` fact definition describes long-term obligations (including current and noncurrent portions of those long-term obligations), not every short-term borrowing. See this [SEC filing's separate long-term and short-term debt disclosures](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/37996/000003799625000147/R70.htm) and the [SEC filing fact definition for `LongTermDebt`](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/33992/000135448814001549/R68.htm).
- Removed that incomplete aggregate fallback. SEC debt now contributes only when current and noncurrent components are both present and match in period and unit; otherwise the existing explicit Yahoo total-debt/fresh aligned pair is used, or debt/EV is unavailable. Added active-analysis regressions for both SEC aliases: a synthetic SEC long-term amount of $50 may not replace Yahoo's total $200, and independently reconciled EV stays **$2,600**.
- Updated the single methodology explanation to state both limitations: long-term-only debt is not a total, and cash including restricted amounts is not an unrestricted EV offset. Focused SEC regressions passed **3/3**; full warnings-as-errors backend suite passed **424/424** in **3.809s** under Python **3.12.15**, using only local mock HTTP fixtures. `git diff --check` and syntax compilation passed. No public providers were contacted. No commit, push, deployment, or production change.

## Exclude restricted-cash aggregate from enterprise value — 2026-10-13 21:31 UTC

- Reproduced a source-definition mismatch: the active SEC cash alias accepted `CashCashEquivalentsRestrictedCashAndRestrictedCashEquivalents` as if it were unrestricted cash, then subtracted the full amount in EV. The [SEC filing fact definition](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/278166/000027816625000128/R44.htm) explicitly includes cash amounts restricted from withdrawal or use. Such amounts may not be available to offset enterprise value.
- Removed the combined restricted-cash alias from the SEC `cash` selector. Explicit `CashAndCashEquivalentsAtCarryingValue` remains eligible; otherwise existing Yahoo cash fallback is used or cash/EV remains unavailable. Added an active-analysis regression where a synthetic SEC combined balance of $500 must not override Yahoo's $100 cash; expected EV independently reconciles as $2,500 market cap + $200 debt − $100 unrestricted cash = **$2,600**.
- Focused SEC regressions passed **3/3**. Full warnings-as-errors backend suite passed **423/423** in **3.813s** under Python **3.12.15**, with local-only mock HTTP fixtures permitted; no public data provider calls. `git diff --check` and syntax compilation passed.
- This changes source selection and can make SEC-only cash and dependent valuation unavailable when unrestricted cash is not separately reported; that is intentional fail-closed behavior, not a new valuation method. No commit, push, deployment, or production change.

## SEC unknown-unit selection hardening — 2026-10-13 21:29 UTC

- An independent fact-selection review reproduced `_sec_candidates()` selecting monetary SEC facts under the `XXX` unit. Currency inference already excluded `XXX`, and active conversion failed closed, so no incorrect value reaching the UI was reproduced. Still, retaining an unknown-currency SEC fact as the selected record was inconsistent and risked misleading provenance or downstream reuse.
- Excluded `XXX` in both preferred-unit and fallback-unit selection, including an explicit `allowed_units={"XXX"}` request. Added a regression proving these paths return unavailable instead of selecting the fact. Reviewed SEC zero-debt component behavior; the apparent concern was disproven because the selector returns a non-empty record containing numeric zero, so current debt aggregation does not drop an explicit zero.
- Focused SEC selector regressions passed **3/3**. Exact warnings-as-errors backend discovery (`python -W error::DeprecationWarning tests/run_all.py`) passed **422/422** in **3.790s** under Python **3.12.15**, with only local mock HTTP fixtures permitted; no public provider traffic. An earlier default-sandbox full invocation failed only because seven local fixture servers could not bind loopback; it is not counted as an application failure. Python syntax compilation and `git diff --check` passed.
- No data-source claim, methodology, UI output, dependency, or external service changed. No commit, push, deployment, or production mutation.

## API and browser-data boundary review — 2026-10-13 21:24 UTC

- A fresh read-only review of active route code and storage flows found no new cross-ticker or cross-sign-in data leak. Analysis coalescing is keyed by normalized ticker, shares only in-flight work, deep-copies follower results, and removes completed/failed flights; authenticated analysis responses are `private, no-store`. Scenario contexts are HMAC-signed and ticker/schema/methodology-bound, then checked for reporting currency, compatible periods, quote freshness, and reconciled market cap/debt/cash. Browser snapshots/drafts use an opaque account scope; snapshots are reduced and retained locally, and the UI explicitly confirms browser-wide clearing. There is no server-side saved-analysis database or completed-analysis cache.
- The independent API/security review also confirmed the existing **P1 rate-limit gap**: `/api/backend-token` can mint new 60-second tokens on each same-origin authenticated request, API dispatch has no account/IP quota, and provider worker/slot limits are process-local. Rechecked the issuer, auth middleware, and worker-cap source; this is not closed by the semaphore or in-flight coalescer. Existing reports/runbook already identify Vercel edge policy or selected durable shared limiting as an operator-controlled gate; no rate-limit service or production WAF rule was added because that requires a provider/plan decision and can change metered production behavior.
- Evidence was source review, existing scenario/auth/coalescer/storage regressions, prior synthetic browser tests, and the current full backend suite (**421/421**) plus frontend suite (**154/154**, Node 22 run). The independent reviewer did not run tests. No public provider was contacted. No app code changed in this review.

## Independent multi-year valuation math regression — 2026-10-13 21:20 UTC

- Added a ten-year constant-growth reverse-DCF check derived from the closed-form geometric-series present value plus Gordon-growth terminal value. It is independent of the engine's annual projection loop and covers discount timing, the terminal-value handoff, and multi-year aggregation. Fixed expected components are **$2,000** forecast present value and **$2,550** terminal present value, totaling **$4,550**. At this fixture growth equals discount; the test handles the ratio-equals-one limit. Engine test module passed **15/15** under Python 3.12.14.
- Initial full-suite attempts did not count as passes: the bundled Python lacked locked runtime packages, and the CI-style run first hit sandbox denial for local mock-server socket binds. Located the already-existing Python 3.12.15 locked environment under `/private/tmp/tsrp-qa-locked-env` and reran the exact warnings-as-errors unittest command with narrowly permitted ephemeral `127.0.0.1` binds. Final backend suite **421/421 passed** in 3.785s, including the new regression. No dependencies were installed and no public provider was contacted.
- No application calculation or methodology changed. Candidate remains detached at `e258c800666ca692785aaee495c4c54b001d3853`; no commit, push, Preview, or production mutation.

## Browser snapshot data minimization — 2026-10-13 21:13 UTC

- Reproduced by code-path inspection: `saveSnapshot()` wrote the entire company analysis to browser local storage, although both saved and current analyses were reduced to the comparison payload before POSTing to `/api/snapshot_diff`. This retained unnecessary company/quote fields and other future response fields on the device.
- New saves now persist the same reduced comparison record required by the comparison route: ticker, retrieval/save dates, signed proof, methodology/schema, score evidence, selected financial metrics and provenance, and scenario assumptions. Existing full snapshots remain readable and are preserved; the privacy notice says they may retain the previous broader payload until removed, pruned by normal per-ticker retention, or cleared. New-record regression confirms `company`, quote price, chart history, and unrelated provider fields are not saved, while values needed by comparison remain identical.
- Frontend tests passed **154/154**, direct strict TypeScript check and ESLint initially passed under Node **24.19.0**; these frontend checks were rerun under the declared Node **22.23.3** in the 21:16 correction below. Isolated production HTTPS mock-OIDC build/integration passed, including updated privacy assertions. Interactive synthetic Chromium flow saved, refreshed, compared, then removed the record; comparison reported the matching snapshot and zero records remained. Yahoo and SEC were disabled. The standard in-checkout Next typegen attempt was blocked by `EPERM` writing the existing `.next/types/routes.d.ts`; direct `tsc` passed and isolated production route generation/build passed.
- No Python code, dependencies, live providers, commit, push, deployment, or production state changed.

## Yahoo debt normalization correction and final verification — 2026-10-13 19:54 UTC

- Independent review found the Yahoo fallback treated `Long Term Debt And Capital Lease Obligation` as total debt. With synthetic same-period cash **100**, current debt **50**, long-term debt **200**, assets **1,500**, and market cap **2,500**, the pre-fix service returned debt **200**, EV **2,600**, and debt/assets **13.33%**; independently expected total debt is **250**, EV **2,650**, and debt/assets **16.67%**. No provider was contacted.
- Fixed the calculation: use Yahoo's explicit `Total Debt` row when available; otherwise sum current and long-term debt only for the exact same statement period and only when both are nonnegative. Never infer total debt from long-term debt alone. The paired components and calculated basis are retained in debt provenance and displayed in source details/exports. Missing or unaligned components leave debt and dependent EV unavailable. Added four regressions; both integration cases failed on the old code and pass after the fix.
- Bumped the active methodology to `score-v7` because corrected inputs can change financial-strength score and valuation. Score weights/equations are otherwise unchanged. Updated active fixtures, snapshot tests, version labels, README, and methodology notes; v6 snapshots are intentionally non-comparable to v7.
- Final checks on Node 22.23.3 / Python 3.12.15: exact CI Python suite **408/408** with deprecation warnings fatal; frontend Node tests **142/142**; ESLint, Next route typegen, TypeScript no-emit, and Next.js 16.4 webpack production build all passed. The final-tree Next.js→Python production-mode integration smoke and HTTPS mock-OIDC journey both passed using synthetic data/secrets and loopback only. These do not verify real providers, real OIDC, or Vercel.
- Review found no competing debt source overwrite: SEC same-period debt remains preferred when valid; Yahoo fallback is used only when SEC is absent/unconvertible/older under the existing selection rules. Verified static UI/export source formatting for calculated debt components. `git diff --check` passed. No provider traffic, reset, commit, push, Preview, or production change.

## Current `braces` advisory applicability — 2026-10-13

- The official [GitHub Advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm) still reports `braces <=3.0.3` affected with **no patched version**; npm still lists 3.0.3 as latest. The candidate lockfile path is `@next/eslint-plugin-next` (dev dependency) → `fast-glob` → `micromatch` → `braces@3.0.3`. No production dependency path appears in the lockfile; `pnpm audit --prod` therefore does not cover this development-only path.
- Correction from installed-plugin source inspection: the current ESLint config does **not** set `settings.next.rootDir`. The plugin's `get-root-dirs` helper defaults to `context.cwd`; it calls `fast-glob.globSync` only when `rootDir` is explicitly a string or array. Thus the vulnerable glob parser is not exercised by the current config through this helper. No app request or financial-data path to brace patterns was found. This narrows observed reachability to a development-tooling dependency that could become reachable if lint configuration changes; it is not an identified deployed application data-plane path. It remains a dependency hygiene/CI-availability risk; do not add an unverified override while upstream provides no patch.

## Yahoo data-rights gate cross-checked against official terms — 2026-10-13

- The active adapter in `backend/research_service.py` uses `yfinance` for Yahoo-backed `Ticker.history`, `Search`, `screen`, and `download` requests. TSRP's privacy notice already states that Yahoo public-display rights have not been cleared.
- Yahoo's [official US Terms of Service](https://legal.yahoo.com/us/en/yahoo/terms/otos/index.html) restrict automated access or collection without express prior permission and restrict some commercial reuse or substitute services. This is a material contract-risk signal, not a legal determination: applicable regional terms and exact project use require qualified legal review. No written permission or licensed replacement is recorded in this repository.
- **Release remains blocked on a provider decision:** retain written permission/licensing that covers automated access and TSRP's display, audience, and distribution, or choose a data source licensed for those uses. No Yahoo endpoint was contacted during this review.

## Latest verified fix — duplicate company-search requests — 2026-10-12 21:08 UTC

- Reproduced an unchanged company query being sent repeatedly when switching research sections. The parent recreated `beginAnalysis` on each render; because `CompanySearch`'s debounced-search effect depended on callbacks derived from that prop, route changes cleaned up and restarted the request. - **Auth logout scope correction (05:57–05:58 UTC):** The OIDC harness previously described sign-out as “revocation,” which overstated what its cookie jar test proves. The current JWT session is stateless: sign-out clears the current browser's session cookie and the test confirms that browser cannot obtain a new API token afterward, but there is no shared durable revocation store. A copied session cookie may remain usable until the 8-hour session expiry; an already-issued backend bearer token may remain usable for its 60-second lifetime. Updated the harness to assert that the browser cookie jar no longer contains the session cookie and to disclaim global revocation. Reran the isolated production HTTPS OIDC integration: **passed, exit 0**. A durable revocation mechanism would require a configured shared store and explicit product/provider decision; none was added.

TSRP's operating-cash-flow-less-capex/provider-FCF proxy is not normalized consistently to either basis, while its discount rate is a static currency/sector assumption without issuer beta, debt cost, tax shield, or capital weights. The code therefore does not establish a conventional FCFF/WACC or FCFE/cost-of-equity valuation. No formula was changed; labels, screen/export copy, provenance, and methodology now call it an illustrative model-required-growth sensitivity and explicitly warn against treating it as market consensus, fair value, or forecast. ## Reporting-period source selection — 2026-10-11 15:16 UTC

- **Reproduced:** the analysis unconditionally preferred an available SEC fact over Yahoo's value, even when SEC's reported period was older. A fixture with SEC revenue of 700 for FY2024 and Yahoo revenue of 1,000 plus period-aligned FCF of 180 for FY2025 returned the stale SEC figure and lost the usable FCF comparison. The older SEC balance sheet could similarly displace newer Yahoo assets/cash/debt.
- **Fixed:** for revenue, net income, assets, equity, cash, and debt, use the newer valid non-future reporting period; SEC remains preferred on the same period. Compare periods before conversion so an unused, older foreign-currency SEC fact cannot trigger avoidable FX requests. Historical-growth freshness now uses the period from the actual history source, not whichever source supplied the headline revenue.
- **Regression coverage:** independently asserted FY2025 revenue/FCF and Yahoo balance figures against the stale SEC fixture; verified the calculated EV (2,600 from 2,500 market cap + 200 debt − 100 cash); verified an older SEC growth series is marked stale when Yahoo provides only one newer annual point; and verified a future-dated Yahoo statement cannot displace current SEC data. Existing current-period SEC and amendment tests remain in the suite.
- **Verification:** complete deterministic Python suite **227/227** passed with Python 3.12.14 and local-only HTTP fixture permission. The locked-package cache omits `soupsieve==2.10`, producing a warning that CSS selectors are unavailable; this is not a clean lockfile environment. The host Python 3.9 run is unsuitable (no `tomllib`) and its loopback fixtures are sandbox-denied. No public financial providers were contacted. `git diff --check` passes.
- Candidate remains detached at `e258c800` with its pre-existing uncommitted work preserved; no publication or production change. ## Scope and status

The candidate's active product is Next.js 16.4 App Router with React/TypeScript UI plus a separate Vercel Python Services backend using the ASGI dispatcher at `backend/api/index.py`; `research_service.py`/`engine.py` is its analytical path. Legacy Streamlit source remains in the repository but is not the active Vercel UI/API path.

| User feature / endpoint | Evidence status |
|---|---|
| TSRP Score, Expectations vs Reality, Valuation, Catalysts & Risks, What Changed? | Deterministic engine/API tests plus synthetic authenticated browser journeys. All five sections and preview Open actions were exercised; delayed analysis/search races preserve the latest ticker. Responsive section containment was checked at 375, 430, 768, 1024, and 1440px. No live-provider journey. |
| Mandatory sign-in and API authorization | Implemented in the candidate: the Next.js page gates research through OIDC; every provider-facing Python route verifies a short-lived server-signed token, and the ASGI boundary authenticates before reading the body. A mocked production HTTPS OIDC flow passes, including state/PKCE/nonce, session cookie, five section URLs, token claims, cross-origin rejection, and sign-out. No TSRP-managed password/profile store is created. Production provider secrets and Preview verification remain required; provider account lifecycle/deletion is handled by the selected identity provider, not TSRP. |
| Company search, analysis, scenario, saved-snapshot comparison | Shared handlers have bounded/strict request parsing and safe errors; scenario math uses validated facts from the displayed analysis without a second provider fan-out; controlled API/financial tests pass. |
| Top movers and top stocks | Separate Yahoo screener paths, provider-failure and pending-timeout recovery fixtures. Headless Chrome exercised one reversed-response market-list race on the candidate at 07:38 UTC: the older result did not replace the newer one and the company/URL stayed unchanged. Live provider behavior and usage rights remain unverified; other visibility/polling interleavings are not exhaustive. |

## Findings and changes

| Priority | Reproduction / cause | Change and evidence |
|---|---|---|
| P2 methodology drift | Static AST comparison of the two application paths confirmed ten identical sector weight/benchmark maps plus an identical default, but `app.py` carried its own full copy. Both copies also stored `discount_rate` values that were never consumed by scoring or valuation; the actual DCF rate comes from `engine.model_rates()` using currency profiles and sector spreads. | The legacy Streamlit path now imports `get_sector_model()` and `has_sector_calibration()` from `methodology.py`, eliminating the duplicated 10-sector map. Removed the unused `discount_rate` field from score-calibration records without changing the engine’s rate formula. New tests require sector weights to normalize, benchmarks to be positive, and rates across every supported currency/sector pair to match the documented formula. The existing score-parity fixtures remain covered by the full suite. |
| P2 boolean financial input | `float(True)` is valid Python, so a malformed boolean from a provider or statement cell was interpreted as `1.0`; directly passing boolean revenue to DCF produced a numeric value. | The shared statement-number parser and active service now reject Python and pandas boolean scalars. The legacy Streamlit numeric wrapper delegates to the same parser, and the standalone engine rejects Python booleans and float-overflow values. Deterministic tests cover revenue/DCF, FCF, dated financial values, and provider-value parsing. |
| P3 calibration state isolation | `get_sector_model()` returned the shared nested module-level mapping directly, allowing any caller to mutate process-wide score weights or benchmarks for later analyses. | The accessor now returns a deep copy. A regression mutates nested weights and benchmarks, then verifies subsequent reads still match the unchanged calibration. |
| P2 malformed sector metadata | The legacy Streamlit path passed Yahoo's sector value directly into calibration and discount-spread lookups. A malformed list/object could crash analysis even when the quote and other facts were usable. | Added bounded shared sector-label normalization; both Python analysis paths use it, non-strings become `Unknown sector`, and unsupported metadata cannot generate a generic score or valuation. A service regression verifies explicit unavailable outputs, the Streamlit `AppTest` has zero exceptions with a list-valued Yahoo sector, and lookup tests cover several malformed values. |
| P2 legacy provider-failure recovery | When the legacy Yahoo quote task raised, `fetch_yahoo_data()` stored an empty pandas DataFrame as its fallback. The subsequent `parts.get("quote") or {}` attempted ambiguous DataFrame truth evaluation and raised instead of returning unavailable quote data; this was reproduced during an earlier import-time failure. | `provider_utils.quote_from_parts()` now accepts only a dictionary and otherwise returns an empty mapping, and the Streamlit loader uses it. Five focused regressions cover valid quotes, the DataFrame failure fallback, malformed shapes, and separate reporting/trading currency preservation. The helper and app syntax pass under host Python 3.9.6 and bundled Python 3.12.14. The full suite passes; the Streamlit app/browser has not been rerun for this patch. |
| P1 legacy currency integrity | `_quote_live()` preferred `fast_info.currency` when populating both `currency` and `financialCurrency`. If a company reports in USD but trades in EUR, the legacy `detect_currencies()` therefore treated financial statements as EUR and could skip a necessary conversion. | `resolve_quote_currencies()` now preserves profile financial currency as reporting currency while using quote/fast-info currency for trading. A deterministic foreign-listing fixture expects USD reporting and EUR trading; fallback cases are also covered. This is a legacy Streamlit adapter; the active Next.js service already keeps these currencies separate. |
| P2 invalid explicit DCF margin | `dcf_enterprise_value()` used `_f(start_margin, fcf_margin)`, so an explicitly supplied boolean, non-finite/malformed value, or nonpositive starting margin could silently fall back or fade into a positive modeled margin and still produce a valuation. API assumptions are validated, so this was primarily a calculation-primitive integrity gap. | The engine now uses the modeled margin only when `start_margin is None`; invalid explicit or nonpositive starting margins return unavailable. Regression cases cover booleans, zero/negative, non-finite values, and malformed strings while confirming omitted `start_margin` still follows the intended fallback. Targeted and full deterministic test suites pass. |
| P1 legacy SEC period integrity | Legacy `annual_filing_records()` grouped SEC rows by fiscal year and filing metadata but did not distinguish duration facts from instants. For a 10-K containing Q4 and FY revenue with the same end, filing date, accession, and fiscal year, the selector kept whichever tied row arrived first; the Q4 amount (e.g. 30) could be presented as annual revenue instead of the full-year amount (e.g. 100). | Added explicit `duration`/`instant` filtering. Revenue, earnings, operating cash flow, and capex select full-year durations; assets, equity, cash, and debt select instant facts. Regression fixtures prove Q4 exclusion, amendment selection, and instant-vs-duration separation. The active web service already applied equivalent duration validation; this fix closes the legacy Streamlit path gap. |
| P1 active financial integrity | A deterministic fixture passed SEC current debt of 100 and noncurrent debt of 200 for the same reporting date/unit. The active service computed 300, then provenance merging replaced the aggregate with the 200 noncurrent component. `choose_sec_or_yahoo()` reads that record's `value`, so final debt was understated by 100 and enterprise value by the same amount. | The selected SEC debt record now explicitly retains the sum and both filing facts. A source-to-analysis regression independently verifies debt 300, cash 300, market capitalization 2,500, and enterprise value 2,500, plus amended revenue, aligned FCF, growth, and score evidence. Typed API validation, the source panel, JSON, and CSV retain/show component provenance; tests cover units, taxonomy, period, filing form/date, and malformed values. No score formula or weights changed. |
| P1 financial integrity (legacy path) | Unknown reporting currencies received the disclosed USD fallback calibration, but the legacy analyzer still called reverse DCF; missing currency metadata also defaulted to USD. It uppercased Yahoo quote subunits such as `GBp`, risking a pence-to-pound identity conversion and mislabeled price. | A shared guarded solver now requires a calibrated reporting currency, supported sector, and positive finite FCF margin in both Python analysis paths. Missing currency stays N/A; legacy quote-currency normalization preserves subunits for display and blocks cross-unit FX. Engine and service tests verify unsupported/missing currency returns no price-based score or reverse-DCF value; the legacy UI itself remains unverified in a browser. |
| P1 financial presentation | Unsupported report currencies could still expose static USD-derived discount/terminal assumptions in returned fields and source metadata; the web client could also show a scenario form that the server would reject. A short-lived older cached response could repeat that presentation. | Unsupported currency now returns null rates, `rate_currency: N/A`, explicit unavailable provenance, and a support flag. The web UI masks old cached rates and hides scenario inputs, rejects scenario-analysis requests, and exports no stale assumption/scenario values. Legacy Streamlit output and evidence tables also show them as unavailable and disable scenarios. New service, runtime-schema, and export regressions cover the states. |
| P2 stale financial periods (legacy app) | The legacy revenue helper rejected future or unlabeled periods but accepted dated statements indefinitely, and did not require a retrieval timestamp. Old annual revenue, free cash flow, or history could therefore continue to feed scoring and valuation. | A single 550-day statement-age limit now comes from `financial_data.py` and is shared with the web service. Legacy revenue and free cash flow require a valid retrieval timestamp and a dated period within that boundary; historical-growth evidence is withheld when its newest period is stale. Missing/invalid timestamps, future periods, and stale statements remain unavailable with a limitation. Boundary tests cover exactly 550 versus 551 days, absent/invalid timestamps, and future periods. |
| P2 export reliability | The download handler revoked its object URL immediately after clicking the temporary link, and export generation was embedded in the UI with no direct parity regression. | Export construction is now a pure helper exercised against score trace, negative FCF, provenance, periods, N/A values, scenario inputs, warnings, and disclaimer. Download cleanup waits one second and removes its temporary link; the test verifies the deferred lifecycle and sanitized filename. CSV native browser delivery is now verified for a synthetic fixture; JSON delivery, forced native failure, and spreadsheet-app opening remain unverified. |
| P2 export interpretation | The valuation UI distinguished exact, below-range, above-range, and unavailable reverse-DCF outcomes, but exports omitted solver bounds/status from JSON and placed the status in CSV's period column. | JSON now includes `solver_status`, `solver_low`, `solver_high`, and `growth_clamped`; CSV has a separate status/bounds column and leaves Period as N/A. A fixture asserts an above-range export retains both bounds and status without presenting them as a period. |
| P2 local API method contract | A real `GET /api/search` returned 405 but incorrectly advertised `Allow: GET, POST, OPTIONS`: the router invoked an unbound route handler with the router instance, so the handler read the router-wide method list. | The router now checks the requested method against the target route's declared allow-list before dispatch. Four real loopback tests cover correct route allowance/cache headers, allowed-origin validation, denied origins, and preflight behavior. The focused HTTP suite and full Python suite pass without provider calls. |
| P2 | API query/body parsing accepted unexpected/duplicate fields, duplicate JSON keys, non-finite numbers, and malformed/truncated input inconsistently. | Shared parsing now bounds input sizes, rejects ambiguous/non-finite/truncated data, and emits generic errors. Route tests cover the contracts. |
| P2 diagnostics/privacy | Logs previously recorded only coarse rejection/error categories, without a request correlation value, route, status, or duration. | Every JSON API response now gets an `X-Request-ID`; one structured line records a generated ID, allow-listed route, method, status, duration, outcome, safe error code, and exception class. Query strings, bodies, IPs, and exception messages are excluded. The UI shows the ID on API errors; local CORS exposes it only to allowed origins. A regression invokes `log_request` on all six Vercel handlers and the local router and confirms the default request-line logger emits nothing. |
| P2 privacy | Company autocomplete included the full typed search in `/api/search?q=...`; [Vercel runtime logs expose request paths and search parameters](https://vercel.com/docs/logs/runtime). | Search now uses a bounded JSON POST body, validates the one expected string field and 80-character limit, sends `Cache-Control: no-store`, and keeps the search text out of TSRP's structured application logs. Tests check valid requests, log omission, invalid fields, and that overlong input never calls Yahoo. Yahoo still receives valid search text to return suggestions; hosting/provider processing and retention remain outside TSRP's control. |
| P2 search failure truthfulness | The bounded provider runner represents a completed provider exception as a missing result. Company search treated that `None` like a successful search with zero matches, including during Yahoo throttling. | Search now maps a missing provider result to its existing safe `search_unavailable` 503. Service and API-handler regressions mock a Yahoo 429, confirm the retryable error, and ensure provider details are not returned; a genuine empty quote list still remains a successful no-match result. |
| P2 provider retry amplification | Both the active service and legacy Streamlit quote reader retried failed `get_info()` calls through `.info`. Inspection of the installed yfinance 1.2.0 confirms `.info` delegates back to `get_info()`, so a failure may repeat the provider read, mask a transient throttle, and increase upstream load. The pinned 1.7.0 package could not be installed locally for source-level verification. | A shared helper now uses `.info` only when `get_info` is absent; both integrations preserve their safe failure behavior without a second read. Regressions cover a throttled first read with a successful property and the legacy-method-absent compatibility path. |
| P2 provenance | The incomplete-balance-sheet risk signal named SEC or Yahoo generically, even if only one field was missing or its source was unavailable. | The signal now identifies cash and debt gaps separately using the corresponding source metadata already present in the analysis. Node tests cover cash-only, debt-only, and mixed source states; no unverified provider is implied. |
| P2 build-tool security | A fresh Python 3.12 test environment inherited `pip 25.0.1`; whole-environment `pip-audit` reported 12 advisory records for that installer, while the declared TSRP runtime requirements were clean. | CI now upgrades pip to at least 26.2.1 before installing packages and audits both `requirements.txt` and the complete installed Python environment. A disposable environment using pip 26.2.1 passed both audits, `pip check`, and all 139 Python tests. The current pip release supports Python 3.12 according to [PyPI](https://pypi.org/project/pip/26.2.1/). The workflow change itself still needs a hosted GitHub Actions run. |
| P2 workflow supply chain | GitHub Actions were referenced by mutable major-version tags. | `checkout` [v7.0.0](https://github.com/actions/checkout/releases/tag/v7.0.0), `setup-python` [v7.0.0](https://github.com/actions/setup-python/releases/tag/v7.0.0), and `pnpm/setup` [v3.0.0](https://github.com/pnpm/setup/releases/tag/v3.0.0) are now pinned to full 40-character upstream commits; a weekly Dependabot policy is configured to propose updates. Full-SHA pinning follows [GitHub's security guidance](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/secure-use#using-third-party-actions). YAML and pin-shape checks are local only; no hosted Actions run has occurred. |
| P3 verification | Public analysis caching is intentional, but the non-cacheable scenario, error, and method-rejection responses lacked explicit cache-policy regressions. | Tests now assert public analysis caching and `Cache-Control: no-store` for scenario success, provider errors, and unsupported methods. Python API-route tests pass. |
| P2 privacy clarity | The README disclosed browser snapshot encryption/sync limits and hosting/provider log visibility, but the in-app privacy expander did not; its search-data wording could also be read as implying all Yahoo calls omit user text. | The in-app disclosure now distinguishes Yahoo search from fixed ticker-screening requests, warns against personal/confidential search input, states SEC request identity, scenario/comparison processing, local-storage retention and deletion limits, and provider/hosting metadata logs. The What Changed? save area also discloses that snapshots are not encrypted, synced, or backed up by TSRP. Lint, typecheck, and production build pass; browser rendering remains unverified. |
| P2 | Corrupt/older browser snapshots could break comparisons, and quota/storage failures were not classified. | Storage preserves valid older records for an honest incompatibility state, caps each ticker at eight, distinguishes corrupt/quota/unavailable states, and requires confirmation before clearing corrupt data. Tests cover retention and failure behavior. |
| P2 | Malformed score traces could throw during comparison serialization; late save comparisons could replace state after a newer request. | Serializer validates old/partial traces; request generations prevent stale comparisons from replacing current results. Regression tests cover malformed and partial inputs. |
| P2 | A refresh failure was hidden behind stale results; users could start overlapping same-ticker refreshes. | Refresh now shows an alert while keeping the previous result identified as stale display, and disables/relabels the control during refresh. This code compiles; browser reproduction remains blocked. |
| P2 | Scenario output could remain visible after assumptions changed or a request was pending. Snapshot comparison could update after clearing/superseding saved research. | Editing assumptions clears/aborts the scenario and invalidates its generation. Snapshot comparisons are generation-guarded; save/remove controls cannot overlap an active comparison. Type/build checks pass; browser interaction remains unverified. |
| P2 resource / financial | Every scenario POST repeated the entire upstream analysis, multiplying Yahoo/SEC requests; client-supplied basis facts could also be altered. | Scenario now signs the minimal source-fact basis with a server-only HMAC key and rejects missing, tampered, wrong-ticker, or key-mismatched contexts before the shared Python DCF engine runs. Scenario requests still make no Yahoo/SEC calls. Deterministic tests cover issuance, verification, tampering, ticker binding, missing/short keys, and the API boundary. Signing detects basis tampering; it is not user authentication, provider verification, or rate limiting. |
| P2 scenario parity | Reverse DCF faded the latest positive reported FCF margin toward the modeled margin, while the scenario calculation held the same displayed modeled margin constant for all ten years. With latest margin 10%, modeled margin 15%, and the same growth/rates, scenario EV was 8.55% higher than the reverse-DCF target. | Version-2 signed scenario context now carries the latest positive reported FCF margin; the scenario uses that as its starting margin and the user's modeled margin as its endpoint. If no positive reported margin exists, it uses the user's margin throughout, consistent with reverse DCF's fallback. The UI explains the margin path. A deterministic independent DCF/solver fixture proves unchanged defaults reproduce enterprise value within tolerance. |
| P2 SEC provider efficiency and error classification | Every analysis fetched the same full SEC ticker-to-CIK map before fetching company facts, and concurrent analyses for one issuer could duplicate the large company-facts request. Concurrent ticker-map failures could also retry sequentially. Malformed SEC JSON could be reported as a network error because Requests' JSON decode exception also inherits from its request-error type. Sharing a company-validated result could also misclassify a second ticker if two map entries shared a CIK but had different titles. | The validated ticker map now has a six-hour process-local success cache and a 30-second typed failure cooldown, returns copies, and coalesces concurrent cold misses. Company-facts downloads are shared only while a request is in flight for the same CIK and SEC contact identity; each caller validates the returned issuer name against its own ticker-map title, and completed facts are deliberately not cached. Controlled concurrency tests cover eight callers, HTTP 429, and same-CIK/different-title identity; the latter preserves success for a matching ticker and `entity_mismatch` for the other. Malformed JSON from either SEC endpoint is now classified as a parse error, with ticker-map cooldown retaining that type. Coalescing remains per-instance only, not shared between Vercel instances. |
| P2 | Company suggestions used combobox semantics without Arrow/Enter selection; navigation scrolled but did not focus the opened heading. | Search supports keyboard option selection/announcement and Escape; section navigation focuses the destination heading, including Open actions targeting the already-open section. Type/build checks pass; screen-reader/browser behavior unverified. |
| P2 state integrity | The typed analysis client checked only the ticker, leaving malformed nested data, non-finite numeric values, out-of-range score values, or an incompatible schema version able to reach render code. | A runtime response guard now checks the current schema version, selected ticker, required financial fields, provenance, score traces and bounds, assumptions, flags, and chart points before updating state. Invalid payloads use a safe retry error. Node fixtures cover valid data, wrong identity, malformed nested values, non-finite chart data, out-of-range scores/weights, and an unsupported schema; lint, typecheck, and production build pass. Browser journey remains unverified. |
| P2 financial | Financial Services and unknown sectors fell through to generic company benchmarks, producing a numeric score and EV/FCF growth result. A controlled fixture produced score 86.10 and implied growth 0.39%; these were synthetic, not live market data. | Version `score-v4` no longer applies unsupported generic calibrations: observed facts remain visible, but score, EV/FCF valuation and scenario are unavailable. The scenario API returns a typed 422. A regression fixture checks each state; old v3 snapshots become incompatible. Existing numerical formulas are unchanged for explicitly calibrated sectors. |
| P2 resource | Python cannot stop a provider request already running when a per-request wait budget expires; per-request executors could multiply worker threads under rapid concurrent requests. | Analysis, FX history, search, and popular-stock history use a 12-worker / 24-task process-local pool. Yahoo screeners use a separate 2-worker / 4-task pool, so slow public lists cannot occupy company-analysis workers. Queued cancellations release capacity; running work retains its slot until completion. Both caps remain per process, not distributed rate limits. |
| P2 public market routes | Strict query parsing treated an empty query string as malformed, so valid no-argument `/api/movers` and `/api/top_stocks` requests returned 400 before reaching their providers. | Empty queries now parse as an empty field map; non-empty queries remain strict. Route regressions verify both no-argument endpoints return 200 and unexpected parameters still return 400. |
| P2 duplicate public-feed work | Simultaneous requests for the same movers or top-stocks list could launch identical provider work; only company analyses used in-flight coalescing. | Both public market-list handlers now share same-key in-flight work within one process. Followers receive independent copies; completed results and failures are not retained. Concurrency and route-wiring regressions pass. This is not cross-instance coordination. |
| P2 Yahoo screener timeout budget | The pinned `yfinance==1.7.0` `screen()` interface has no timeout argument. Its predefined screener calls `YfData.get()` with the 30-second default; the previous 12/25-second future waits did not stop an already-running thread. | **Fixed locally:** market tasks now receive a submission-time deadline propagated into the same supported curl session; each request uses the remaining task budget (maximum 8 seconds), and expired work cannot start another request. Most-active list budget is 12 seconds; the concurrent gainers/losers pair uses 25 seconds. A delayed-market-worker regression verifies no late request after caller timeout. The pool is still process-local and this does not establish Yahoo rights, a distributed rate limit, or live provider reliability. |
| P2 public API abuse | `/api/analyze`, `/api/search`, `/api/movers`, and `/api/top_stocks` can cause upstream provider work without a request-rate limit. The executor bounds only concurrent tasks in one warm process; it does not cap repeated requests or coordinate Vercel instances. | Confirmed by read-only route/config review. A reliable distributed limit needs Vercel Firewall configuration or a separately selected shared limiter; no in-memory counter is presented as a cross-instance fix. Vercel's Firewall can rate-limit, but this project has no configured rule, the dashboard change can affect production immediately, and rate-limit use is metered beyond applicable included usage. No external rule or service was enabled without explicit cost/production authorization. |
| P2 financial (legacy app) | The older Streamlit analysis path assigned the fixed missing-evidence value to absent score factors and did not gate generic sector fallbacks. | It now uses evidence-weighted score/expectations helpers, shows N/A below the tested 70% overall evidence threshold, withholds score and EV/FCF outputs for unsupported sectors, and versions saved comparisons as `score-v4`. Engine tests exercise missing and partial evidence; local AppTest covers the current app runner. Visual/browser and hosted Community Cloud behavior remain unverified. |
| P2 financial (legacy history/current-value consistency) | The Streamlit adapter discarded SEC revenue period ends and used row-count CAGR; skipped fiscal years could be treated as adjacent periods. A final selector review also found current SEC revenue/provenance excluded amended forms while history accepted them, so an amended period could disagree between displayed revenue and historical growth. | One shared annual-filing selector now chooses the latest filed 10-K/20-F/40-F, including amendments, per fiscal year for both current SEC revenue/provenance and historical series. The legacy analyzer uses the existing gap-aware dated CAGR helper. Fixtures prove current-row selection and historical observations both choose the same amendment; malformed/non-annual rows and missing-year gaps remain covered. AppTest with gapped Yahoo annual statements reaches Expectations vs Reality with zero app exceptions. |
| P2 financial (legacy revenue period) | Reproduced with a Yahoo quote containing `totalRevenue` but no dated income statement: the old path treated the summary number as latest annual revenue and attributed it to Yahoo/SEC. Revenue statement labels also were not validated as actual, non-future dates before score and valuation use. | The legacy analyzer now accepts SEC or Yahoo revenue only with a verifiable period-end date no later than retrieval; future, year-only, and synthetic labels are rejected. The unperioded quote-summary fallback is excluded, EV/Sales is unavailable without a current dated revenue value, and the Score, Expectations, and Valuation views explain the limitation. Deterministic helper tests and mocked AppTest cases verify summary-only N/A, future-period rejection, and dated-statement availability. |
| P1 financial-period integrity (web and legacy paths) | `period_observations` sorted only if every provider column parsed. One malformed label left 2024 ahead of 2025; a year-only label such as `2026` parsed as January 1 and allowed a false aligned FCF of 20. Conflicting duplicate periods could also pair one revenue observation with a different same-date cash-flow observation. Both legacy SEC history and the active web SEC selector could accept year-only period/filing dates. | Statement observations now require complete date labels, discard malformed/synthetic columns, normalize and sort valid dates independently, and exclude periods with conflicting duplicate values. Both legacy annual facts and active SEC instant/duration selection require full end/filed dates before selecting facts or amendments. Legacy latest-value/history helpers share strict observations; cash-flow history uses period-aligned FCF. Yahoo/SEC date regressions pass; service logic also passes syntax compilation under Python 3.12. A mocked no-ticker Streamlit AppTest reports zero app exceptions/errors; company analysis and visual layout remain untested by that smoke check. |
| P1 quote/FX period ordering and chart dates | When Yahoo omitted `regularMarketTime`, analysis used the final rows in provider order for quote price, prior close, and market timestamp. Unsorted history with one malformed date produced price **118** and prior close **999** instead of the actual latest chronological bars **120 / 118**. FX and popular-stock fallback shared that assumption. UTC-normalizing exchange-local midnight bars also shifted some Asian-market chart dates back one calendar day. | History is sorted by normalized UTC instants while retaining the provider's original exchange-local index. Chart labels serialize as calendar dates rather than UTC instants. Controlled regressions cover malformed/unordered bars, analysis price fallback, FX selection, and preservation of a Tokyo trading date. No live Yahoo call was made. |
| P2 chart adjustment labeling | The chart's screen-reader description said “adjusted closing prices,” but the API requests `auto_adjust=False` and plots Yahoo's `Close` column rather than `Adj Close`. | The chart description now calls the series daily closing prices and explicitly says it is not dividend-adjusted. The analysis fixture gives `Close` and `Adj Close` different values and verifies the plotted API series uses `Close`; the frontend production build and ESLint pass. |
| P2 source attribution (legacy app) | AppTest showed `pick_value(None, None, ..., "Yahoo Finance")` returned an unavailable value with a Yahoo source label; the Expectations footer also listed SEC unconditionally, and growth/export rows hardcoded both providers. | Missing fallbacks now have no source. Historical-growth provenance follows the selected series; consensus and reverse-DCF sources are labeled only when values exist. Expectations/Valuation footers and export rows use metric-specific provenance and show Unavailable when absent. Mocked AppTest verified summary-only and dated Yahoo cases without a false SEC citation. |
| P2 financial comparison (legacy Expectations) | The Revenue growth row compared analyst growth with past CAGR but displayed a difference calculated from reverse-DCF growth minus the analyst estimate. The reverse-DCF row reused that same difference despite showing past CAGR; at the solver boundary this produced an exact-looking `+109.9 pts` beside a `>120%/yr` value. | Revenue-growth comparisons no longer subtract different horizons. The reverse-DCF row now compares its required-growth value to past CAGR, and only when the solver returned an in-range result; boundary/out-of-range differences remain N/A with an explicit status. A mocked AppTest fixture verifies the prior false point-gap does not appear. |
| P2 provider resilience (legacy chart) | The Streamlit fallback line chart allowed missing OHLC fields, but volume coloring still indexed `Open` unconditionally and could crash on partial history. | Volume colors now use neutral tone when open/close values are absent, mismatched, equal, or non-finite. Two pure helper tests cover valid direction and unavailable inputs; AppTest exercised all five sections using Close/Volume-only chart history without exceptions. |
| P2 financial (compatibility helpers) | The exported `growth_reality_score`, subscore wrappers, and `score_breakdown` still substituted a fixed 40 for missing data, despite having no live application callers. | They now delegate to the evidence-aware score-v4 helpers; missing/non-finite evidence stays unavailable, while complete-data fixture outputs and weights remain unchanged. Regression tests cover the old wrapper entry points. |
| P2 currency/display | Python correctly refused to convert listing quote subunits into reporting-currency market value, but the UI currency formatter also rendered valid `GBp`/`GBX`/`ZAc`/`ILA` share prices as `N/A`. | The formatter now prints the numeric amount with its exact subunit code and performs no conversion. A mocked LSE analysis verifies the share-price source remains `GBp` while market cap, enterprise value, required growth, and score remain unavailable without a valid conversion. |
| P2 provider reliability | Empty or malformed Yahoo company metadata could be reported as a ticker-not-found 404; malformed optional statement payloads, throttling, and per-call exceptions lacked direct recovery assertions. | Empty/invalid identity metadata and Yahoo 429-style failures now yield retryable 503 responses. Optional malformed statements remain missing, while valid quote data can still return as an honest partial analysis. Tests cover malformed metadata/statement shapes, safe throttle errors, and FX throttling producing no cross-rate. |
| P2 stale market list | The Yahoo “Popular stocks” fallback accepted date-only history up to eight calendar days old, while the browser treated date-only session labels as UTC-midnight instants and filtered them after 72 hours. The API could return an apparently successful list that the UI silently discarded; an independent review also found an edge at UTC midnight. | The API now limits fallback dates to two calendar days. The browser compares date-only bars to the validated response retrieval date, so an accepted session date stays stable across midnight. Exact-timestamp mover quotes now use the same 48-hour and five-minute-skew policy as company quote freshness; date-only history remains a separate two-calendar-day fallback rule. Tests cover both boundaries, invalid/future dates, midnight crossover, stale responses, and stale-provider fallback. |
| P2 malformed chart response | The client accepted any string as a chart date, although range selection assumes parseable dates; duplicate source sessions could also produce repeated chart labels. Malformed data could reach rendering or cause the strict chart contract to reject otherwise valid company research. | The client now requires real `YYYY-MM-DD` dates in strict ascending, unique order. The service sorts valid session dates and omits ambiguous duplicate dates and invalid closes. Python fixtures cover malformed/out-of-order/duplicate rows; TypeScript fixtures cover invalid calendar dates, duplicates, and unordered points. An independent review confirmed the contract matches backend output. |
| P2 partial market-list disclosure | `daily_market_movers()` deliberately retained a successful gainers or losers screener when its pair failed, but the response and ticker UI gave no indication that the ranking was incomplete. | The response now includes a fixed, non-provider-derived coverage note naming the unavailable group; the UI renders it only for the movers list. Complete results have no warning, top-stocks state merging preserves it, and failed refreshes clear it. Python regressions cover complete data, provider exceptions, and task-cap rejection. |
| P2 ticker polling race (static finding) | The five-minute polling effect had no cancellation or latest-response guard, so a delayed earlier response could replace newer list data and in-flight work survived unmount. | Added independent latest-request gates for movers and top-stocks requests. A new poll aborts its predecessor; only the current request can update state; cleanup aborts both. Two helper tests verify superseding and cancellation. Delayed component-level browser responses remain unverified. |
| P2 ticker response validation | Client-side checks accepted a response timestamp arbitrarily far in the future and were embedded in the UI, leaving timestamp edge cases without deterministic coverage. | Extracted a pure validator. It now rejects response timestamps over five minutes ahead or older than fifteen minutes, exact-timestamp quotes over five minutes ahead or older than the shared 48-hour market-data window, malformed/non-finite entries, and preserves provider order with a seven-item cap. Date-only popular-stock history continues to use its separate two-calendar-day rule. |
| P1 scenario consistency | A four-day-old quote could still produce a signed scenario context in the analysis response even though scenario submission correctly rejected that stale basis. | Analysis now calls the same scenario-basis eligibility validator as scenario submission before advertising the signed token, and returns explicit `available`, `not_configured`, `unsupported`, or `unavailable` status. The UI only opens editable assumptions when a valid context exists and explains unavailable states. API tests cover current, stale, unsupported-sector, and missing-key cases; response-schema tests reject status/token contradictions. |
| P1 financial provenance (legacy enterprise value) | A controlled Yahoo quote supplied an intentionally absurd, undated `enterpriseValue` alongside dated balance-sheet inputs. The legacy path accepted that summary value before its component calculation, so a stale or differently denominated provider aggregate could feed valuation. | The legacy analyzer now ignores the undated Yahoo EV summary and derives EV from current market capitalization plus same-period debt less cash, after currency conversion and comparability checks. A tested helper rejects missing, negative, non-finite, and overflowing components while preserving a mathematically negative EV. Tests cover arithmetic and invalid boundaries; a fixture-backed AAPL AppTest with all outbound requests blocked had zero app exceptions/rendered errors and confirmed the absurd summary value was absent from the rendered analysis. |
| Disproven candidate — scenario reconciliation overflow | Review raised the possibility that large finite inputs could make derived EV infinite and bypass `inf > inf`. The API already bounded each financial input to `1e16`, so this overflow cannot be reached through the validated payload. | No valuation behavior change was needed. Named the existing limit `MAX_SCENARIO_FINANCIAL_AMOUNT` and added a regression that sends 1.01× the limit for revenue, market cap, EV, cash, and debt; all are rejected as unavailable before reconciliation. |

| P2 CSV formula injection (web export) | OWASP notes full-width formula starters may be evaluated in some spreadsheet locales. Before the fix, the web CSV serializer emitted full-width `＝ ＋ － ＠` and BOM-prefixed formula-like strings unchanged; a regression reproduced this. | The string-cell guard now detects ASCII and full-width formula prefixes after Unicode whitespace and prefixes the exported text; typed numeric negatives remain unchanged. Unit and research-export integration regressions pass. Spreadsheet save/reopen behavior was not tested; OWASP warns apostrophe/quote mitigations may be removed by Excel and no universal sanitizer is safe for every spreadsheet/downstream consumer. See [OWASP CSV Injection guidance](https://owasp.org/www-project-web-security-testing-guide/latest/4-Web_Application_Security_Testing/07-Injection/21-CSV_Injection/). |
| P2 first-load content visibility | A production HTTP request to the statically generated route returned the title and a 2px loading bar only. `useSearchParams()` inside the page-wide Suspense boundary caused the rest of the app to be client-rendered, leaving the initial HTML without the search form or research guide until hydration. | The page now waits for the incoming request with Next.js `connection()`, allowing the existing URL-state client component to render on the server without duplicating navigation state. Added a production HTTP smoke check for homepage content and the `?ticker=AAPL&section=Valuation` deep link, and connected it to CI after the production build. The smoke passes on the disposable production build. Manual hydration, browser history, and cross-runtime Vercel behavior still require browser/Preview verification. |

## Score-response finding — P2

- The frontend previously accepted a finite company score even when it disagreed with the returned score trace and did not reconcile component contributions/weights. A fixed 80/70/90 fixture with normalized 7/17, 6/17, 4/17 weights reproduced acceptance of a deliberately altered company total before the fix.
- The response validator now requires score availability and total agreement, normalized base/applied weights, unique component keys, weighted evidence coverage, and per-component contribution arithmetic. It rejects malformed trace states before UI updates; it does not calculate or replace the engine score. The regression fails before and passes after the fix.

## Latest score-contract verification — 2026-10-10 09:15 UTC

- Focused schema suite **7/7**; all Node tests **50/50**; full Python suite **236/236** with deprecations as errors; ESLint; TypeScript no-emit; `git diff --check`; Next.js **16.4.0 webpack** production build and production HTTP smoke for homepage plus AAPL/Valuation deep link all passed against the resulting source. Build/smoke used a disposable source copy; no generated build files were added to the checkout.
- The new test was run before the validator fix and failed on the mismatched score case; it passed after the fix. No market provider was called.
- Usage at 09:15 UTC: 6% used in the 5-hour window, 35% weekly, and three free reset credits available. Neither window met the 5%-remaining threshold; no reset or paid usage was used.

## Current provider and deployment documentation review — 2026-10-10 09:22 UTC

- Rechecked current official Vercel docs. They explicitly support existing projects with file-based Python functions under `/api`, including `BaseHTTPRequestHandler`, and describe `vercel.json` function configuration. They also recommend Services for a Python backend alongside another framework; Services are now documented as beta on all plans, not as requiring an access request. TSRP's root Next.js preset plus per-function Python config is structurally consistent with the documented file-function pattern, but the actual linked project's build/routing remains unverified because this checkout has no `.vercel` settings and a CLI build stops before building with `project_settings_required`. README wording was corrected; no speculative routing migration was made. Sources: [Python functions in `/api`](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/python/api-directory), [Vercel Services](https://vercel.com/docs/services), [Vercel function configuration](https://vercel.com/docs/project-configuration/vercel-json).
- The current Yahoo general Terms of Service state that automated data collection requires express prior permission and restrict creating a material substitute/competing data service; the `yfinance` project says its Yahoo data access is intended for personal use. TSRP actively uses `yfinance` for quotes, statements, estimates, search, FX, and screeners, and the repository has no evidence of a Yahoo grant covering this public app. This remains a public-release stop-ship pending written permission or a licensed replacement and qualified review; this is a risk finding, not a legal opinion. Sources: [Yahoo Terms of Service](https://legal.yahoo.com/us/en/yahoo/terms/otos/index.html), [`yfinance` legal notice](https://github.com/ranaroussi/yfinance#legal-stuff).
- Rechecked SEC guidance: SEC documents a maximum 10 requests/second and asks automated clients to declare a real User-Agent contact; TSRP keeps SEC unavailable until `SEC_USER_AGENT` is configured. Source: [SEC Webmaster FAQ](https://www.sec.gov/about/webmaster-frequently-asked-questions).
- A read-only browser reattempt found the Vercel and TSRP tabs but could not open a page because the admin-enforced browser policy could not be verified. No browser, network, or deployment workaround was used; responsive UI verification remains blocked.

## Yahoo screener timeout-boundary audit — 2026-10-10 09:26 UTC

- Inspected the exact installed `yfinance==1.7.0` source from the isolated dependency environment. Its public `screen()` interface has no caller-supplied timeout; its `YfData.get/post` transport defaults to a 30-second request timeout, with a cookie/crumb step and HTTP-error strategy fallback. The project waits 25 seconds for gainers/losers and 12 seconds for most-active results. Those are soft caller deadlines: a running worker is not killed and holds its semaphore slot until the library returns; the process-local screener pool caps workers at two and outstanding tasks at four. This is bounded concurrency, not a strict end-to-end provider deadline.
- Added deterministic regressions for one pending losers request (retain completed gainers, label incomplete coverage, request cancellation) and a pending most-active request (use the explicitly labeled popular-stock fallback). Focused market-mover suite **17/17**; full Python suite **238/238** on both host and isolated Python **3.12.14**, with deprecations as errors. No live provider call was made and runtime code was unchanged.
- A hard per-call network deadline cannot be passed through the pinned public screener interface. Avoided a process-global/private yfinance monkey patch or a new provider without permission. A strict transport deadline remains dependent on selecting a supported, rights-cleared adapter; the current safe behavior is to return partial/unavailable data while bounded worker capacity recovers.

## Release blockers / remaining checks

- **Stop-ship — Yahoo data rights (reviewed 2026-10-10):** Yahoo's current U.S. English [Terms of Service](https://legal.yahoo.com/us/en/yahoo/terms/otos/index.html) say automated collection requires express prior permission and restrict using Yahoo content to create a competing or substitute service. The [`yfinance` project notice](https://github.com/ranaroussi/yfinance#legal-stuff) says the Yahoo Finance API is intended for personal use and directs users to Yahoo's terms for data rights; the Apache license covers the library, not the downloaded market data. TSRP currently fetches and publicly displays search, quotes, screeners, estimates, and fallback statements. Confirm the terms applicable to the operator and intended user jurisdictions; obtain written permission for this specific use or replace the feed with a licensed provider, then obtain appropriate legal review. This is a release-risk finding, not a legal opinion.
- SEC requests are unavailable until `SEC_USER_AGENT` uses the operator’s real, monitored contact. Configure only after confirming the identity; do not fabricate it. Rechecked 2026-10-11 15:34 UTC: it remains unset in this shell; `.env.example` leaves it blank.
- The provider-task cap is local to one warm function process. Vercel instances do not share it; no distributed abuse/rate limiter is configured. The cap may fail fast with explicit unavailable provider values under per-instance saturation.
- Yahoo HTTP calls, including screeners, use a tested bounded curl-cffi session with an 8-second per-request ceiling and a propagated total task deadline that prevents late retries. The Python caller cannot forcibly kill a running worker thread, so capacity remains occupied until its bounded transport returns; the previous “no per-call timeout” blocker is resolved in deterministic transport tests. Data rights remain unresolved independently.
- **Public API rate limiting remains a stop-ship security/availability control.** The provider-backed routes `/api/analyze`, `/api/search`, `/api/movers`, and `/api/top_stocks` have no shared limit in code. The official [rate-limit documentation](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/rate-limiting), checked 2026-10-10, confirms all-plan availability but per-region counters; Hobby provides one rule per project, IP/JA4 keys, fixed windows, and 1,000,000 allowed requests, while published pricing is $0.50 per million allowed requests. Confirm account plan and usage before configuration. WAF rules must be configured in Firewall rather than the documented `vercel.json` challenge/deny subset, and publishing them affects production immediately. No rule was created or published because this task does not authorize production changes or potential usage charges. See also [WAF usage and pricing](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/usage-and-pricing) and [custom-rule actions](https://vercel.com/docs/vercel-firewall/vercel-waf/custom-rules).
- Scenario base facts are now HMAC-signed at analysis time and verified by the API before calculation; this detects client-side basis edits but does not re-fetch or independently validate provider data. Results are not persisted or shared. Configure the server-only signing secret and platform-level rate limiting before public exposure. Rechecked 2026-10-11 15:34 UTC: `TSRP_SCENARIO_SIGNING_SECRET` is unset in this local shell, so scenario analysis remains disabled here.
- `backend/uv.lock` now locks the resolved Python runtime graph and hashes; `uv lock --check` passed. The latest locked Python 3.12.14 suite passes **275/275**. A final-lock `pip-audit` previously reported no known vulnerabilities (point-in-time result, not a security certification). This does not establish the Vercel Linux bundle, cold-start behavior, or managed installer behavior; those require a linked Preview. `yfinance` 1.7.0 remains Beta and its provider/data rights remain unresolved.
- This candidate uses Vercel Services in `vercel.json`: Next.js frontend service plus Python service rooted at `backend/`, ASGI entrypoint `api.index:app`, and `/api/*` rewrites to that backend. Rechecked official Vercel documentation on 2026-10-10: the current `services` configuration replaces legacy `experimentalServices`; Services are marked Beta, and a project only builds in Services mode when its dashboard Framework is set to Services and `vercel.json` contains `services`. This candidate's current `services` block and top-level ordered rewrites match the documented shape, and the Python 3.12/custom module entrypoint is supported by its runtime docs. The actual project setting and deployment routing remain unverified because no project link/CLI is available. See [Vercel Services setup and routing](https://vercel.com/kb/guide/vercel-services), [Services reference](https://vercel.com/docs/services), and [Python runtime and entrypoints](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/python).
- Current official Next.js installation docs set Node.js 20.9 as the minimum; TSRP declares Node `>=22` in `package.json`, `.nvmrc` selects 22, and CI uses Node 22. This clears the documented framework minimum by configuration, but the linked Vercel project's actual Node setting/build output remains unverified. Next.js documents a Node.js server as supporting all Next.js features; the local production build does not establish the mixed Vercel routing. ## Server-aligned freshness clock — 2026-10-10 07:59 UTC

## ASGI body bounds and timeout UX — 2026-10-10 12:44 UTC

- The Services adapter now accepts POST requests whose ASGI server omits `Content-Length`, applies a bounded 30-second body-read deadline, and rejects oversized chunks before copying them into the request buffer. A stalled body gets the shared safe JSON `408` response instead of hanging the function. Regression cases previously failed on a 400 and an unbounded wait, respectively.
- Added a frontend API-client regression proving the structured 408 message remains recoverable and includes only the safe request reference ID. Node **22.23.3** frontend suite: **58/58 passed**; focused API-client suite: **6/6 passed**. Provider terms, service persistence, privacy disclosures, and any SMTP cost require owner setup/review.
- Supabase's current Next.js SSR instructions require cookie-backed sessions and verified claims; it warns against trusting raw session cookies. Its JWT docs require signature and claim checks and describe the project JWKS endpoint. The Python service would need a separately tested bearer-token verifier, issuer/audience/expiry checks, safe key caching/rotation, and negative tests on every API route. A decorative sign-in page or frontend-only redirect would not meet the requirement.

## Yahoo HTTP wait-budget containment — 2026-10-11 13:36 UTC

- **Reproduced:** the analysis worker wait budget is 20 seconds, but cancelling a running `Future` does not stop its thread. The active lock pins yfinance 1.7.0; its `Ticker.info` uses two serial data calls whose default underlying request timeout is 30 seconds each. A slow provider can therefore retain scarce worker capacity after the caller has received an error. This was a bounded-capacity degradation risk, not an unbounded thread leak.
- **Fixed:** a custom session using yfinance's supported curl transport now caps each Yahoo HTTP request at 8 seconds (including calls whose library default is 30 seconds) and is used consistently by company, FX, search, screener, and historical-download paths. Chrome impersonation is preserved. The already-transitive `curl-cffi==0.16.3` is declared directly and locked. Four regression tests verify the cap and that pinned yfinance accepts/shares the session without making network calls.
- **Verified:** Python suite **214/214** under Python 3.12.15 with deprecations as errors and local-only HTTP test permission; Node **81/81**; ESLint; strict TypeScript no-emit; `uv lock --check --offline`; locked production dependency export; isolated Next.js 16.4.0 webpack production build; `git diff --check`. No public financial-data provider was contacted.

## Yahoo total-request deadline propagation — 2026-10-11 13:40 UTC

- The original fix bounded each Yahoo HTTP request at 8 seconds. Final review showed that a sequence of provider calls could still exceed the enclosing wait, so submission-time deadlines are now propagated through provider worker threads. Each request uses the smaller of the 8-second transport cap and remaining task time; when the deadline is expired, retries raise before another request is opened. Queued work does not receive a new budget when a worker becomes available.
- Seven focused tests cover caps, connect/read tuples, actual `session.get` transport interception, expired-deadline denial, worker deadline propagation, late-retry denial after caller timeout, and pinned yfinance session acceptance. Full backend suite: **217/217** under Python 3.12.15 with deprecations treated as errors and the local HTTP fixture enabled.

## API abuse throttling requires deployment-level configuration — 2026-10-11 19:04 UTC

A rule template defaults to logging and must be changed to deny to enforce. - **Release gate:** the Vercel project owner must decide whether to enable and configure a per-IP WAF rule for the `/api/*` research routes, review its costs, set thresholds, and verify it blocks traffic. ## ASGI disconnect cancellation and current-source production smoke — 2026-10-11 21:51 UTC

- Independent review confirmed disconnects after route dispatch were not observed. ASGI now watches for disconnects while synchronous route work runs, signals a thread-safe request cancellation event, returns without fabricating a response, and cancels queued provider work. Provider orchestration polls for that signal and refuses to start later Yahoo requests after cancellation. Already-running synchronous transfers cannot be forcibly interrupted; their existing per-request and total deadlines still bound resource use. This limitation remains explicit and covered by a test.
- A newly exercising ASGI test exposed that the test-only HTTP bridge incorrectly emitted `http.disconnect` immediately after every request body. Corrected the bridge to leave an open connection pending; the unit test injects a real disconnect message directly. Also moved declared oversized-body rejection into the ASGI boundary, before reading or dispatching the body, preserving the “reject without reading” contract.
- The cancellation regression also issues a fresh request after the aborted one and confirms the worker-thread cancellation context is cleared rather than leaking across reused executor threads.
- Verification on current source: after the final disconnect race-path adjustment, complete backend suite **341/341** passed; current frontend suite **98/98**, ESLint, TypeScript no-emit, and `git diff --check` passed. Retain this as a dev/CI supply-chain risk for explicit owner acceptance or upstream/toolchain resolution; do not report it as fixed.
