## SEC User-Agent identity validation — 2026-10-12

- **Finding / P2 configuration-integrity issue:** `_sec_identity()` accepted any `SEC_USER_AGENT` containing an email address. A contact email by itself therefore enabled SEC requests without identifying the client. SEC's official [Accessing EDGAR Data](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-search-assistance/accessing-edgar-data) guidance illustrates a company name plus contact email; the SEC API toolkit also expects a vendor/software identifier. This was established from the active predicate and provider-call path; no live SEC request was made.
- **Fix:** Require a non-empty client/organization name before exactly one email, no trailing text, no control characters, and a bounded header length. Invalid values return the existing `not_configured` state before SEC network access. Updated `.env.example`, README, Privacy & data notice, and the SEC source-panel copy; all now state that TSRP validates format only and cannot certify truthfulness or monitoring.
- **Regression evidence:** Tests cover valid identity, missing name/email, email-only, malformed/trailing text, multiple emails, control characters, excessive length, and no SEC calls on invalid configuration.
- **Verification:** Python/API **359/359** with warnings as errors; frontend Node **106/106**; ESLint passed; isolated current-source Next.js 16.4.0 webpack type generation, strict TypeScript no-emit, and production build passed. The in-checkout `pnpm typecheck` could not write protected `.next/types/routes.d.ts`; its equivalent typegen+tsc passed in a disposable copy excluding environment files and secrets. Final `git diff --check` is pending after this report update.
- No real contact identity, credential, or secret was added. No SEC/Yahoo request, deployment, commit, push, or production change occurred.

### 2026-10-12 02:16 UTC — bounded local request-body recovery and SEC FCF alignment coverage

- Independent API review identified that the local threaded development adapter had no socket read timeout; an authenticated, allowed-origin POST that declared a body and then stalled could keep a request thread blocked. Set a 30-second per-connection timeout and map body-read timeouts to a safe HTTP 408. A real loopback regression using a synthetic token returned `408 request_timeout` in about 103 ms with a test-only 0.1-second timeout.
- Independent financial review found missing focused assertions, not a production logic defect: SEC free-cash-flow records are already rejected unless operating cash flow, capital spending, and revenue have matching period, start date, and unit. ### Period-mismatched SEC currency conversion — 2026-10-12 03:31 UTC

- **Reproduced financial-integrity defect:** SEC annual and balance-sheet facts in a currency different from the issuer reporting currency were converted using Yahoo's latest five-day spot quote, regardless of the SEC observation period. The pre-fix fixture used FY revenue ending 2025-12-31 and a 2026-10-01 USD/EUR rate, and the analysis accepted the resulting converted SEC value. The same helper also translated instant balance-sheet facts at current FX.
- **Fix:** SEC facts are now converted only for a validated same-unit identity. Cross-currency SEC facts fail closed until a period-aligned conversion source is implemented. Existing Yahoo facts remain eligible as a separately sourced fallback; otherwise the value stays unavailable. A quality warning states that period-aligned FX is unavailable and current spot rates are not used for historical facts. Current market capitalization still uses current FX, which is period-appropriate for a current market value.

It asserts the Yahoo figures and sources are used, no USD/EUR spot lookup is made for the historical facts, and the warning is present. ### Market-value FX timestamp alignment — 2026-10-12 03:40 UTC

- **Reproduced data-integrity gap:** a 36-hour-old USD/EUR quote passed the FX adapter's 72-hour freshness window, even though the equity market quote was current. That conversion could feed market capitalization, enterprise value, and dependent valuation/score inputs while claiming a market timestamp unrelated to the FX observation.
- **Fix:** current market-cap conversion now requires every FX leg to be timestamped no more than 24 hours before its market quote (with a five-minute clock-skew allowance). Missing, malformed, or misaligned timestamps make the converted market cap unavailable; EV and dependent outputs then remain unavailable. The exact reason appears once as a quality warning. Same-currency identity requires no FX timestamp. The existing quote-relative regression uses a fresh market quote and a 36-hour-old FX quote; helper tests cover the 24-hour boundary, five-minute future tolerance, malformed/missing dates, and the valid listing-currency conversion path.
- **Verification:** after this change and warning deduplication, full Python 3.12.14 backend suite with warnings as errors: **367/367 passed** in the existing cached environment (not exact-lock: `soupsieve` 3.0.1 vs locked 2.10). No external market-data or production requests. Frontend suite previously passed **108/108**, with ESLint and strict TypeScript checks; frontend files have not changed since those checks.
- Rebuilt a sanitized temporary copy of the current source with Next.js 16.4.0 webpack and an empty environment; production build passed. The production HTTP smoke then passed setup/auth gating, deep links, privacy, synthetic signed analysis through Next.js → Python ASGI, search/snapshot routes, input rejection, and headers. Yahoo/SEC were mocked or unavailable; no external services were called. - Final frontend checks after that test-harness change: Node tests **108/108**, ESLint exit 0, strict TypeScript no-emit exit 0. `git diff --check` passed after code and report edits.

SEC states that government-created SEC.gov and EDGAR public-filing content is free to access and reuse; the separate operational requirements include efficient moderated access, no more than 10 requests/second in aggregate per user, and a declared User-Agent. TSRP's `_sec_identity()` requires an organization/client label and one contact email, but cannot establish that the contact is monitored. `SEC_USER_AGENT` remains operator-supplied and blank in `.env.example`; do not invent it. The API's process-local coalescing/cache does not guarantee a cross-instance SEC request ceiling, so deployment-wide control still needs verification. Sources: [SEC Webmaster FAQ](https://www.sec.gov/about/webmaster-frequently-asked-questions), [SEC Developer Resources / Fair Access](https://www.sec.gov/about/developer-resources).
- This review clarifies the difference between SEC content reuse and SEC request-policy compliance, and between a library license and rights to Yahoo data. It does not change code, data providers, configuration, or release disposition. No live provider request, deployment, production change, commit, or push was made.

### Reject ambiguous/non-standard SEC JSON — 2026-10-12 04:54 UTC

- **Reproduced and fixed a provider-boundary integrity defect:** Python's default JSON decoder accepts non-standard `NaN`/`Infinity` constants, can overflow a valid exponent such as `1e9999` into `inf`, and silently keeps the last occurrence of duplicate object keys. The streamed SEC decoder now rejects all of these, causing the existing SEC adapter to return its typed `parse_error` state instead of treating non-finite or ambiguous response data as available.
- Added an integration regression through `_fetch_sec_company_facts()` for `NaN`, `Infinity`, exponent overflow, and duplicate top-level `facts` keys. Each asserts a typed parse error and response closure. Focused SEC tests passed **27/27** before the additional numeric edge; the new targeted cases passed **3/3** afterward. Complete backend suite passed **368/368** on the final code under the project's full runner. It required explicit permission for two test-only ephemeral loopback sockets; the unprivileged attempt failed only at those socket binds and was not counted. No live SEC, Yahoo, or FX requests were made.
- The test process imported `pandas 2.3.3`, `curl_cffi 0.16.3`, and `yfinance 1.7.0`. No dependency or lockfile changed. Python backend changed; frontend code did not, so frontend **109/109**, ESLint, TypeScript, and the fresh Next build remain the preceding 04:52/04:50 evidence. Candidate Preview, Node 22, Yahoo data rights, SEC identity/aggregate-rate controls, and independent methodology/legal review remain open.
- No reset or paid usage, commit, push, deployment, external provider request, or production change.

### Enforce SEC deadline after JSON decoding — 2026-10-12 04:59 UTC

### API request-body framing regression — 2026-10-13 22:17 UTC

- The ASGI adapter previously used Python's permissive `int()` parsing for Content-Length before the stricter route helper rejected malformed values. It now validates ASCII decimal syntax before consuming a body, normalizes valid zero-padded lengths, and safely bounds oversized decimal declarations without an unbounded integer conversion.
